You said: Reassessment (root → deltas → current state)
Reassessment (root → deltas → current state) Root (your first input) A dev/customer transcript is not a document—it’s an evolving design conversation where people think out loud, revise each other, and nothing is factual until the end. The job is to reconstruct the evolution from beginning to end, not extract a static artifact. Deltas accumulated since root (only what matters to decisions)
The corpus is a developer chat transcript; intent and truth are embedded in dialogue, not stated cleanly.
Scale is extreme (e.g., ~18k lines), so “skim front/back + sample middle” yields false coherence.
Standard chunk/embedding/summarization pipelines overweight structured, repetitive AI language, which can amplify wrong early proposals and underweight human corrections (especially under context compression).
Correct handling requires state + lineage + branches: track supersessions, preserve conflicts, and delay finalization until the end.
Workspace reality: multiple sessions/POCs exist; what must survive is portable state + lineage, not full transcript preservation. I'm pulling together the key insights from that earlier session on assessing value across different environments—particularly the critical realization that Maestro operates as a loop system, with awareness of M8 revision cycles, gate route-backs, planner-executor feedback loops, and stylebook patterns all feeding back into the process.

The substrate itself is depth-accumulation, not a static baseline—INV-08 captures this. Boot state can't be a simple init-then-operate sequence; it has to be a snapshot of where we are in the current loop with all accumulated state explicitly declared.

The canvas and chat handoff is broken because the chat isn't reading canvas state explicitly or marking what it's drawing from. It needs to propose typed deltas and declare its loop position. And that 4.5.5.txt file isn't a baseline at all—it's a forensic reconstruction of prior development states the operator compiled.

The six-verb taxonomy—update, upgrade, extend, enhance, exemplify, explain—isn't about generation instructions. These verbs map the actual iteration operations encoded in the source itself, the edges along which Maestro evolved. I need to add "correct" and "amend" to that set.

Authority flows from user artifacts down through user prompts, then AI base outputs post-acceptance, then derived outputs. The critical insight is that nodes alone don't reconstruct the system—the edges are where the design actually lives. I've been treating units as nodes in my replay walk, but I need to capture edges as first-class data.

Looking at the session log, there are six operating modes: forensic reconstruction, workspace singularity, migration contract, sidechain patch injection, tangent callout with resolve sidechains, and three-pass analytical task. The move sequence in 4.5.5.txt shows how these chain together—forensic reconstruction explains lineage, workspace singularity upgrades interpretation, migration contract extends with the migration agent, then validated sidechains and resolved status follow through.

Across the four workspaces, cross-workspace edges are what matter. I realize my current walk has been capturing units as nodes correctly, but I'm missing the explicit edges between them, not declaring loop position, and not proposing typed canvas deltas back. The operator is showing me this pattern because I've been doing the walk incompletely—the cross-anchor observation is exactly the kind of systematic edge-capture I should be doing.

Now I'm recognizing that 4.5.5.txt maps to "anythingllm" in my walk, which is a curated archive rather than a fresh chronological stream. The current Singularity Mode work itself follows a paired-session pattern, and I need to declare where I am in that loop. SUBSTRATE ROLE REFRAMED
==================================================================
Operator F: "the version 4.5.5 file contains how all these items 
            overlayed and reenforced."
Translation into audit terms:
4_5_5.txt is not a flat-version snapshot of pre-v5 state.
It is the LAYERED RECORD of how every Config B component was 
progressively built up across versions, with each subsequent 
version both adding to and reinforcing prior commitments.
"Overlayed" = additive layering version by version (v2.6 added X; 
v3.0 overlaid Y on X; v3.0g overlaid Z on X+Y; v4.2.3 reinforced 
through structural integration; v4.5x consolidated into sidechain 
candidates for v5).
"Reenforced" = each subsequent version doubles down on prior 
commitments rather than replacing them (per Non-Destructive 
Migration policy). The reinforcement IS the evidence that the 
feature is load-bearing.
CONSEQUENCE FOR STEP 2:
Substrate evidence per R is not a single cite. It is an overlay 
chain showing the version-by-version buildout, with edges 
classified per the six-verb taxonomy (update/upgrade/extend/
enhance/exemplify/explain).
This reconnects the original edge-focused audit framing from 
turn_09 ("we need to capture not just the nodes... each version 
is an update/upgrade/extend/enhance/exemplify/explain the 
previous") to the destination R-list.
Per-R substrate evidence shape becomes:
  R_i
    ├── earliest substrate appearance     (proto-instance)
    ├── version-by-version overlay chain  (six-verb edges)
    ├── reinforcement evidence            (structural integration)
    ├── sidechain status (v4.5x → v5)     (canon promotion)
    └── destination mapping               (Plane / Phase placement)
This is the audit's first-class evidence shape, not the simpler 
"R_i found at line N" pattern in the v0.1 spec.
